06F | Ledger: team timeout and handoff contract drift, post-hoc check

Read the full outcome matrix before this selected trace

Read-only historical evidence. Original commands are never executed by the archive. Prepared exploratory task. Reservation reviewer independently discovers real defects, but the single baseline also passes; reviewer scratch-file and synthesis caveats remain. Ledger team times out before integration and fails a labeled post-hoc contract check despite passing the original checker. Scheduling has passing behavior with scope/lifecycle failures. See the full Workshop 6 audit.

New implementation exercise; stubs deliberately incomplete. Implement codec.py encode(fields) and decode(line), reducer.py summarize(lines). fields is a list of strings, length >=1. Encode joins fields with | and escapes backslash as \\ and pipe as \|. decode reverses this, preserves empty and Unicode fields, and raises ValueError for a trailing backslash or an escape other than backslash or pipe. Empty line decodes to ['']. summarize consumes iterable encoded records [event_id, account, signed_integer_cents]. Each record has exactly 3 fields, cents matches optional minus sign followed by one or more ASCII digits (leading zeros allowed, + disallowed). Account and ID may be empty or contain escaped delimiters. Exact retransmissions (same decoded ID/account/integer cents) count once. Conflicting duplicate IDs raise ValueError. Malformed encoding, field count or cents raises ValueError. Return dict account -> summed integer cents, retaining zero totals; empty input -> {}. Use codec.decode in reducer. Do not mutate input. Only codec.py, reducer.py, test_ledger.py may change. No dependency installs.

Split on unescaped `|`. Preserve empty fields and all Unicode characters exactly. An empty line decodes to `['']`. Raise ValueError on a trailing backslash (backslash as the last char of line) and on any escape sequence other than `\\\\` or `\\|` (e.g. `\\x`). Be case-sensitive and exact; no stripping of whitespace/newlines inside decode.\n- Both functions must handle strings only. Keep them dependency-free (stdlib only).\n\nVerification (mandatory): verify with in-memory Python probes ONLY (e.g. `python3 -c '...'` or a heredoc piped to python3, no file left behind). Cover at minimum:\n1. encode([\"a\",\"b\"]) == \"a|b\"\n2. encode([\"a|b\",\"c\\\\d\"]) == correct escaped form and decode round-trips to the exact original list.\n3. decode(\"a|b\") == [\"a\",\"b\"]; decode(\"\") == [\"\"]; decode(\"|\") == [\"\",\"\"]; decode(\"||\") == [\"\",\"\",\"\"].\n4. Round-trip (decode(encode(F)) == F) for: unicode fields (\"héllo|wörld\", \"日本語\", emoji), fields containing backslash and pipe in various orders, empty fields, single field [\"\"], field that is \"\\\" and field that is \"|\".\n5. decode(\"abc\\\\\") raises ValueError (trailing backslash); decode(\"a\\\\xb\") raises ValueError (invalid escape); decode(\"a\\\\|b\") == [\"a|b\"]; decode(\"a\\\\\\\\b\") == [\"a\\\\b\"].\n6. encode([\"\\u2603\"]) (snowman) == \"\\\\u2603\"? Empty line decodes to ['']. ...\\\"\\n\\nTherefore the codec.py interface you must provide:\\n- encode(fields: list[str]) -> str: join fields with '|'; within each field escape backslash as `\\\\\\\\` and pipe as `\\\\|`. Escaping must be left-to-right so that backslashes produced by earlier escapes are not re-escaped (e.g. a raw field `\\\\|` must encode to exactly the 4 chars `\\\\\\\\\\\\|`, i.e. backslash backslash backslash pipe, and round-trip back to `\\\\|`).\\n- decode(line: str) -> list[str]: exact inverse of encode. Split on unescaped `|`. Preserve empty fields and all Unicode characters exactly. An empty line decodes to `['']`. Raise ValueError on a trailing backslash (backslash as the last char of line) and on any escape sequence other than `\\\\\\\\` or `\\\\|` (e.g. `\\\\x`). Be case-sensitive and exact; no stripping of whitespace/newlines inside decode.\\n- Both functions must handle strings only. Keep them dependency-free (stdlib only).\\n\\nVerification (mandatory): verify with in-memory Python probes ONLY (e.g. `python3 -c '...'` or a heredoc piped to python3, no file left behind). Cover at minimum:\\n1. encode([\\\"a\\\",\\\"b\\\"]) == \\\"a|b\\\"\\n2. encode([\\\"a|b\\\",\\\"c\\\\\\\\d\\\"]) == correct escaped form and decode round-trips to the exact original list.\\n3. decode(\\\"a|b\\\") == [\\\"a\\\",\\\"b\\\"]; decode(\\\"\\\") == [\\\"\\\"]; decode(\\\"|\\\") == [\\\"\\\",\\\"\\\"]; decode(\\\"||\\\") == [\\\"\\\",\\\"\\\",\\\"\\\"].\\n4. Round-trip (decode(encode(F)) == F) for: unicode fields (\\\"h\\u00e9llo|w\\u00f6rld\\\", \\\"\\u65e5\\u672c\\u8a9e\\\", emoji), fields containing backslash and pipe in various orders, empty fields, single field [\\\"\\\"], field that is \\\"\\\\\\\" and field that is \\\"|\\\".\\n5. decode(\\\"abc\\\\\\\\\\\") raises ValueError (trailing backslash); decode(\\\"a\\\\\\\\xb\\\") raises ValueError (invalid escape); decode(\\\"a\\\\\\\\|b\\\") == [\\\"a|b\\\"]; decode(\\\"a\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\b\\\") == [\\\"a\\\\\\\\b\\\"].\\n6. encode([\\\"\\\\u2603\\\"]) (snowman) == \\\"\\\\\\\\u2603\\\"? No \\u2014 check it is the literal snowman character back in decode; i.e. Unicode passes through untouched, only backslash and pipe are special in encode.\\n\\nReport back concisely:\\n- The exact interface signatures you implemented.\\n- The list of probes (input -> expected -> observed) and pass/fail for each, including the ValueError cases.\\n- Any assumptions or edge-case decisions.\\nKeep the report under ~600 words.   "prompt": "Workspace: /private/tmp/odsc-workshop6-v2/ledger-team-t1\n\nREADME.md there is the complete acceptance contract. Your job: implement ONLY the file reducer.py (function `summarize(lines)`). It must use `codec.decode` from codec.py (the import is already at the top of the stub). Do NOT edit codec.py or test_ledger.py, and do NOT create any new files. Use `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in all shell commands. Do not run git commands, do not install anything, no network.\n\nContract (verbatim README clauses relevant to you):\n\"summarize consumes iterable encoded records [event_id, account, signed_integer_cents]. Each record has exactly 3 fields, cents matches optional minus sign followed by one or more ASCII digits (leading zeros allowed, + disallowed). Account and ID may be empty or contain escaped delimiters. Exact retransmissions (same decoded ID/account/integer cents) count once. Conflicting duplicate IDs raise ValueError. Malformed encoding, field count or cents raises ValueError. Return dict account -> summed integer cents, retaining zero totals; empty input -> {}. Use codec.decode in reducer. Do not mutate input.\"\n\nThe team interface for codec (agreed, from README — codec.py may still be a stub on disk while you work; DO NOT rely on its on-disk state):\n- codec.decode(line: str) -> list[str]: inverse of encode; splits on unescaped '|'; preserves empty and Unicode fields; raises ValueError on trailing backslash or escape other than '\\\\' or '|'; decode(\"\") == [\"\"].\nBehavior requirements for summarize(lines):\n- lines is any iterable of encoded record strings (list, generator, or file-like). Handle a trailing single newline per line: if a line ends with '\\n', drop it; then if it ends with '\\r', drop it (so \"a|b|c\\n\" and \"a|b|c\" are equivalent). Do NOT mutate or consume the input iterable beyond iterating it.\n- Decode each line with codec.decode; propagate any ValueError from decode unchanged.\n- After decoding there must be exactly 3 fields: [event_id, account, cents_field]; otherwise raise ValueError.\n- cents_field must fully match `-?[0-9]+` (ASCII digit regex, not \\d): optional ASCII minus then one or more ASCII digits. Leading zeros allowed, '+' disallowed, anything else (empty, spaces, '.', unicode digits, letters) -> ValueError. Convert cents_field to Python int.\n- event_id and account may be empty and may contain escaped delimiters (they come back already unescaped from decode). Preserve them exactly as the decoded values.\n- Exact retransmission = same decoded event_id AND same account AND same integer cents; count once (do not add twice).\n- Conflicting duplicate ID: same decoded event_id appearing with a different account OR a different integer cents (including e.g. \"10\" vs \"-10\", \"10\" vs \"7\") -> raise ValueError. Note \"5\" and \"05\" both equal integer 5, so same id + same account + \"5\" then \"05\" is an exact retransmission, NOT a conflict.\n- Result: dict mapping account -> summed integer cents across all unique records. Accounts that appear must remain in the dict even when their total is 0 (\"retaining zero totals\"). Empty input -> {}.\n- Do not change any input structure/list; strings are immutable so just don't write to whatever you're given.\n\nIMPORTANT verification approach (you cannot rely on codec.py being implemented): verify your reducer with in-memory probes by injecting a reference `codec` module into sys.modules inside the python heredoc, e.g.:\n\n```\nimport sys, types\nref = types.ModuleType(\"codec\")\ndef _enc(f): ...\ndef decode(line): ...  # reference implementation matching the agreed interface above\nref.decode = decode\nsys.modules[\"codec\"] = ref\nimport reducer\n```\n\nDo NOT write this reference into any file and do NOT alter the on-disk codec.py. (If by the time you run, codec.py on disk already implements the interface, you may additionally run an integration probe against the real codec at the end for confidence, but your correctness must stand with the injected module.)\n\nCover at minimum these probes against summarize:\n1. summarize([]) == {}\n2. single record, e.g. [\"e1|acct|10\"] -> {\"acct\": 10}\n3. multiple records summing: [\"a1|x|5\",\"a2|x|3\",\"a3|x|-8\"] -> {\"x\": 0} (zero retained)\n4. empty account: [\"e0||5\",\"e1||7\"] -> {\"\": 12}; empty id: [\"|x|9\"] -> {\"x\": 9}\n5. escaped delimiters: id \"a\\b\", account \"North|South\", cents \"-100\" encoded via the reference encoder -> decoded account key \"North|South\" with -100\n6. leading zeros: [\"e|x|0007\"] -> {\"x\": 7}; unicode cents \"١٢\" (Arabic digits) -> ValueError\n7. cents validation: \"5\" ok, \"-5\" ok, \"\" ValueError, \"+5\" ValueError, \"5.0\" ValueError, \"x\" ValueError, \"5 \" ValueError\n8. field count: \"a|b\" and \"a|b|c|d\" -> ValueError; malformed encoding line like \"a\\\\x|b|c\" -> ValueError (from decode)\n9. exact retransmission: [\"e|x|10\",\"e|x|10\",\"e|x|010\"] -> {\"x\": 10}\n10. conflicts: [\"e|x|10\",\"e|y|10\"] -> ValueError; [\"e|x|10\",\"e|x|-10\"] -> ValueError; [\"e|x|5\",\"e|x|05\"] -> OK {\"x\":5}\n11. input immutability: pass a list, confirm after the call the list is unchanged.\n12. iterable input: a generator expression works; trailing-newline variant \"e|x|10\\n\" and \"e|x|10\\r\\n\" equal \"e|x|10\".\n\nReport back concisely:\n- The exact summary of your summarize implementation (structure, not full code).\n- Each probe (input -> expected -> observed) and pass/fail, including the ValueError cases.\n- Your assumptions (especially newline-stripping and the \"5\"/\"05\" decision).\nKeep the report under ~700 words. You

Workspace: /private/tmp/odsc-workshop6-v2/ledger-team-t1

README.md there is the complete acceptance contract. Your job: implement ONLY the file codec.py (functions `encode(fields)` and `decode(line)`). Do NOT edit reducer.py or test_ledger.py, and do NOT create any new files (no scratch files, no probes on disk). Use `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in all shell commands. Do not run git commands, do not install anything, no network.

Contract (verbatim from README):
"Implement codec.py encode(fields) and decode(line), reducer.py summarize(lines). fields is a list of strings, length >=1. Encode joins fields with | and escapes backslash as \\ and pipe as \|. decode reverses this, preserves empty and Unicode fields, and raises ValueError for a trailing backslash or an escape other than backslash or pipe. Empty line decodes to ['']. ..."

Therefore the codec.py interface you must provide:
- encode(fields: list[str]) -> str: join fields with '|'; within each field escape backslash as `\\` and pipe as `\|`. Escaping must be left-to-right so that backslashes produced by earlier escapes are not re-escaped (e.g. a raw field `\|` must encode to exactly the 4 chars `\\\|`, i.e. backslash backslash backslash pipe, and round-trip back to `\|`).
- decode(line: str) -> list[str]: exact inverse of encode. Split on unescaped `|`. Preserve empty fields and all Unicode characters exactly. An empty line decodes to `['']`. Raise ValueError on a trailing backslash (backslash as the last char of line) and on any escape sequence other than `\\` or `\|` (e.g. `\x`). Be case-sensitive and exact; no stripping of whitespace/newlines inside decode.
- Both functions must handle strings only. Keep them dependency-free (stdlib only).

Verification (mandatory): verify with in-memory Python probes ONLY (e.g. `python3 -c '...'` or a heredoc piped to python3, no file left behind). Cover at minimum:
1. encode(["a","b"]) == "a|b"
2. encode(["a|b","c\\d"]) == correct escaped form and decode round-trips to the exact original list.
3. decode("a|b") == ["a","b"]; decode("") == [""]; decode("|") == ["",""]; decode("||") == ["","",""].
4. Round-trip (decode(encode(F)) == F) for: unicode fields ("héllo|wörld", "日本語", emoji), fields containing backslash and pipe in various orders, empty fields, single field [""], field that is "\" and field that is "|".
5. decode("abc\\") raises ValueError (trailing backslash); decode("a\\xb") raises ValueError (invalid escape); decode("a\\|b") == ["a|b"]; decode("a\\\\b") == ["a\\b"].
6. encode(["\u2603"]) (snowman) == "\\u2603"? No — check it is the literal snowman character back in decode; i.e. Unicode passes through untouched, only backslash and pipe are special in encode.

Report back concisely:
- The exact interface signatures you implemented.
- The list of probes (input -> expected -> observed) and pass/fail for each, including the ValueError cases.
- Any assumptions or edge-case decisions.
Keep the report under ~600 words. Do not describe making any changes to files other than codec.py.

Encode joins fields with | and escapes backslash as \\ and pipe as \|. decode reverses this, preserves empty and Unicode fields, and raises ValueError for a trailing backslash or an escape other than backslash or pipe. Empty line decodes to ['']. summarize consumes iterable encoded records [event_id, account, signed_integer_cents]. Each record has exactly 3 fields, cents matches optional minus sign followed by one or more ASCII digits (leading zeros allowed, + disallowed). from codec import encode, decode

checks = []
def expect(name, cond):
    checks.append((name, bool(cond)))

expect("e1", encode(["a","b"]) == "a|b")
expect("e2", encode(["a|b","c\\d"]) == "a\\|b|c\\\\d")
expect("e2rt", decode(encode(["a|b","c\\d"])) == ["a|b","c\\d"])
expect("d3", decode("a|b") == ["a","b"])
expect("dempty", decode("") == [""])
expect("dpipe", decode("|") == ["",""])
expect("d2pipe", decode("||") == ["","",""])

roundtrips = [
    ["héllo|wörld"], ["日本語"], ["\U0001F600"], ["a\\b"], ["a|b|"],
    ["\\|", "|\\"], ["\\"], ["|"], ["a\\|b", "x\\\\y"],
    ["", ""], [""], ["\\\u2603|\\|"],
]
for F in roundtrips:
    expect("rt:"+repr(F), decode(encode(F)) == F)

def raises(line):
    try:
        decode(line)
        return False
    except ValueError:
        return True
expect("v1", raises("abc\\"))
expect("v2", raises("a\\xb"))
expect("v3single", raises("\\"))
expect("e3", decode("a\\|b") == ["a|b"])
expect("e4", decode("a\\\\b") == ["a\\b"])

README.md there is the complete acceptance contract. Your job: implement ONLY the file reducer.py (function `summarize(lines)`). It must use `codec.decode` from codec.py (the import is already at the top of the stub). Do NOT edit codec.py or test_ledger.py, and do NOT create any new files. Use `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in all shell commands. Do not run git commands, do not install anything, no network.

Contract (verbatim README clauses relevant to you):
"summarize consumes iterable encoded records [event_id, account, signed_integer_cents]. Each record has exactly 3 fields, cents matches optional minus sign followed by one or more ASCII digits (leading zeros allowed, + disallowed). Account and ID may be empty or contain escaped delimiters. Exact retransmissions (same decoded ID/account/integer cents) count once. Conflicting duplicate IDs raise ValueError. Malformed encoding, field count or cents raises ValueError. Return dict account -> summed integer cents, retaining zero totals; empty input -> {}. Use codec.decode in reducer. Do not mutate input."

The team interface for codec (agreed, from README — codec.py may still be a stub on disk while you work; DO NOT rely on its on-disk state):
- codec.decode(line: str) -> list[str]: inverse of encode; splits on unescaped '|'; preserves empty and Unicode fields; raises ValueError on trailing backslash or escape other than '\\' or '|'; decode("") == [""].
Behavior requirements for summarize(lines):
- lines is any iterable of encoded record strings (list, generator, or file-like). Handle a trailing single newline per line: if a line ends with '\n', drop it; then if it ends with '\r', drop it (so "a|b|c\n" and "a|b|c" are equivalent). Do NOT mutate or consume the input iterable beyond iterating it.
- Decode each line with codec.decode; propagate any ValueError from decode unchanged.
- After decoding there must be exactly 3 fields: [event_id, account, cents_field]; otherwise raise ValueError.
- cents_field must fully match `-?[0-9]+` (ASCII digit regex, not \d): optional ASCII minus then one or more ASCII digits. Leading zeros allowed, '+' disallowed, anything else (empty, spaces, '.', unicode digits, letters) -> ValueError. Convert cents_field to Python int.
- event_id and account may be empty and may contain escaped delimiters (they come back already unescaped from decode). Preserve them exactly as the decoded values.
- Exact retransmission = same decoded event_id AND same account AND same integer cents; count once (do not add twice).
- Conflicting duplicate ID: same decoded event_id appearing with a different account OR a different integer cents (including e.g. "10" vs "-10", "10" vs "7") -> raise ValueError. Note "5" and "05" both equal integer 5, so same id + same account + "5" then "05" is an exact retransmission, NOT a conflict.
- Result: dict mapping account -> summed integer cents across all unique records. Accounts that appear must remain in the dict even when their total is 0 ("retaining zero totals"). Empty input -> {}.
- Do not change any input structure/list; strings are immutable so just don't write to whatever you're given.

IMPORTANT verification approach (you cannot rely on codec.py being implemented): verify your reducer with in-memory probes by injecting a reference `codec` module into sys.modules inside the python heredoc, e.g.:

```
import sys, types
ref = types.ModuleType("codec")
def _enc(f): ...
def decode(line): ...  # reference implementation matching the agreed interface above
ref.decode = decode
sys.modules["codec"] = ref
import reducer
```

Do NOT write this reference into any file and do NOT alter the on-disk codec.py. (If by the time you run, codec.py on disk already implements the interface, you may additionally run an integration probe against the real codec at the end for confidence, but your correctness must stand with the injected module.)

Cover at minimum these probes against summarize:
1. summarize([]) == {}
2. single record, e.g. ["e1|acct|10"] -> {"acct": 10}
3. multiple records summing: ["a1|x|5","a2|x|3","a3|x|-8"] -> {"x": 0} (zero retained)
4. empty account: ["e0||5","e1||7"] -> {"": 12}; empty id: ["|x|9"] -> {"x": 9}
5. escaped delimiters: id "a\b", account "North|South", cents "-100" encoded via the reference encoder -> decoded account key "North|South" with -100
6. leading zeros: ["e|x|0007"] -> {"x": 7}; unicode cents "١٢" (Arabic digits) -> ValueError
7. cents validation: "5" ok, "-5" ok, "" ValueError, "+5" ValueError, "5.0" ValueError, "x" ValueError, "5 " ValueError
8. field count: "a|b" and "a|b|c|d" -> ValueError; malformed encoding line like "a\\x|b|c" -> ValueError (from decode)
9. exact retransmission: ["e|x|10","e|x|10","e|x|010"] -> {"x": 10}
10. conflicts: ["e|x|10","e|y|10"] -> ValueError; ["e|x|10","e|x|-10"] -> ValueError; ["e|x|5","e|x|05"] -> OK {"x":5}
11. input immutability: pass a list, confirm after the call the list is unchanged.
12. iterable input: a generator expression works; trailing-newline variant "e|x|10\n" and "e|x|10\r\n" equal "e|x|10".

Report back concisely:
- The exact summary of your summarize implementation (structure, not full code).
- Each probe (input -> expected -> observed) and pass/fail, including the ValueError cases.
- Your assumptions (especially newline-stripping and the "5"/"05" decision).
Keep the report under ~700 words. Do not describe changes to any file other than reducer.py.

Tool result: terminal

def encode(fields):
    return "|".join(
        field.replace("\\", "\\\\").replace("|", "\\|")
        for field in fields
    )


def decode(line):
    fields = []
    current = []
    i = 0
    n = len(line)
    while i < n:
        c = line[i]
        if c == "|":
            fields.append("".join(current))
            current = []
            i += 1
        elif c == "\\":
            if i + 1 >= n:
                raise ValueError("trailing backslash")
            nxt = line[i + 1]
            if nxt == "\\":
                current.append("\\")
                i += 2
            elif nxt == "|":
                current.append("|")
                i += 2
            else:
                raise ValueError("invalid escape")
        else:
            current.append(c)
            i += 1
    fields.append("".join(current))
    return fields

New implementation exercise; stubs deliberately incomplete. Implement codec.py encode(fields) and decode(line), reducer.py summarize(lines). fields is a list of strings, length >=1. Encode joins fields with | and escapes backslash as \\ and pipe as \|. decode reverses this, preserves empty and Unicode fields, and raises ValueError for a trailing backslash or an escape other than backslash or pipe. Empty line decodes to ['']. summarize consumes iterable encoded records [event_id, account, signed_integer_cents]. Each record has exactly 3 fields, cents matches optional minus sign followed by one or more ASCII digits (leading zeros allowed, + disallowed). Account and ID may be empty or contain escaped delimiters. Exact retransmissions (same decoded ID/account/integer cents) count once. Conflicting duplicate IDs raise ValueError. Malformed encoding, field count or cents raises ValueError. Return dict account -> summed integer cents, retaining zero totals; empty input -> {}. Use codec.decode in reducer. Do not mutate input. Only codec.py, reducer.py, test_ledger.py may change. No dependency installs.

ref = types.ModuleType("codec")
def encode(fields):
    return "|".join(f.replace("\\","\\\\").replace("|","\\|") for f in fields)
def decode(line):
    fields=[]; cur=[]; i=0; n=len(line)
    while i<n:
        c=line[i]
        if c=="|":
